Countless figures have been called Antichrist, including [Holy Roman Emperor Frederick II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor_Frederick_II "Holy Roman Emperor Frederick II"), Russian emperor [Peter the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_the_Great "Peter the Great"), [Saladin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saladin "Saladin"), Pope [John XXII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_XXII "John XXII"), [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini "Benito Mussolini"), [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"), French Emperor [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon "Napoleon") Bonaparte, German Führer [Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler"), and US President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump").[[178]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-178)[[179]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-179)[[180]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-180)[[181]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-181)[[182]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-APantichristTrump-182)
### Anti-Paganism
See also: [Witch-cult hypothesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witch-cult_hypothesis "Witch-cult hypothesis")
It is speculated that the [witch trials of the Early Modern period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witch_trials_in_the_early_modern_period "Witch trials in the early modern period") were an effort to suppress [pre-Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_ancient_Rome "Religion in ancient Rome"), [pagan religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paganism "Paganism") that had endured the [Christianization of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianization "Christianization"). Some far right conspiracy theorists suggest that [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") was created as a Jewish agenda to undermine the [Aryan race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_race "Aryan race"), which is thought to have initially led to the [fall of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Rome "Fall of Rome").[[148]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-:1-148)
During the 1930s and 1940s, neo-Pagan [Heinrich Himmler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinrich_Himmler "Heinrich Himmler") organized a branch of the [SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schutzstaffel "Schutzstaffel") to do an extensive survey of witch-hunt trial records across Europe, with the intention to use it as [anti-Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Christian_sentiment "Anti-Christian sentiment") propaganda by claiming the inquisition had been a repression of an indigenous _[Völkisch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%B6lkisch_movement "Völkisch movement")_ Norse-Germanic nature religion, and using it as evidence for reconstructing that religion.[[183]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-183)
### Anti-Islamic
Main article: [Islamophobic trope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic_trope "Islamophobic trope")
Since the [September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks"), many [anti-Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobia "Islamophobia") conspiracy theories have emerged. [Love Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Jihad "Love Jihad"), also called Romeo Jihad, refers to a conspiracy theory that Muslim men try to convert non-Muslim women to Islam by feigning love.[[184]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-184)[[185]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-185)[[186]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Common-186)[[187]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-:3-187) The "[Eurabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurabia "Eurabia")" theory alleges a Muslim plot to [Islamize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamization "Islamization") Europe and the West through mass immigration and high birth rates.[[188]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-188) US president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") was accused of being a [secret Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_religion_conspiracy_theories "Barack Obama religion conspiracy theories").[[189]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-189)
### Bible and Jesus
[Bible conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_conspiracy_theory "Bible conspiracy theory") posit that significant parts of the [New Testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Testament "New Testament") are false, or have been omitted. Various groups both real (such as the [Vatican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_See "Holy See")) and fake (such as the [Priory of Sion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priory_of_Sion "Priory of Sion")) are said to suppress relevant information concerning, for example, the dating of the [Shroud of Turin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shroud_of_Turin "Shroud of Turin").[[190]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-190) Much of this line of conspiracy theory has been stimulated by a debunked book titled _[The Holy Blood and the Holy Grail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holy_Blood_and_the_Holy_Grail "The Holy Blood and the Holy Grail")_ (1982), which claimed that [Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus "Jesus") and [Mary Magdalene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Magdalene "Mary Magdalene") were lovers and that their offspring and descendants were secretly hidden in Europe following the death of Jesus, from whom the then-living French [draughtsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drafter "Drafter") [Pierre Plantard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Plantard "Pierre Plantard") claimed descent. Interest in this hoax saw a resurgence following the publication of [Dan Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Brown "Dan Brown")'s 2003 novel _[The Da Vinci Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Da_Vinci_Code "The Da Vinci Code")_.[[191]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-191) _[The Gospel of Afranius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Gospel_of_Afranius "The Gospel of Afranius")_ , an atheistic Russian work published in 1995 with an English translation published in 2022, proposes politically motivated [gaslighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaslighting "Gaslighting") as the origin of the foundational Christian belief in the [resurrection of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resurrection_of_Jesus "Resurrection of Jesus").[[192]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-192)
### Islamist
"[War against Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_against_Islam "War against Islam")" is a conspiracy theory in [Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism "Islamism") discourse that describes a plot to destroy [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam") society. The alleged plotters are [non-Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafir "Kafir") and "[false Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munafiq "Munafiq")" in collusion with Western powers whose efforts are a continuation of the Medieval [Crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades "Crusades").[[193]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-193)
### Paul the Apostle
[Paul the Apostle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle "Paul the Apostle") makes an appearance in some variants of the medieval Jewish anti-Christian polemic, _[Toledot Yeshu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toledot_Yeshu "Toledot Yeshu")_ , as a spy for the [rabbis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbi "Rabbi").[[194]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-194) Muslims have long believed that Paul purposefully corrupted the [original](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesus_in_Islam "Jesus in Islam") revealed teachings of Jesus[[195]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHindsonCaner2008280-195)[[196]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Young200460-196)[[197]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTERiddell2001235-197) by introducing elements of [paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paganism "Paganism"),[[198]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg1999276-198) the theology of [the cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atonement_in_Christianity "Atonement in Christianity"),[[199]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWaardenburg1999255-199) and the idea of [original sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_sin "Original sin") producing the need for [redemption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redemption_\(theology\)#Christianity "Redemption \(theology\)").[[200]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Young200464-200)
### Racism
[White genocide conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_genocide_conspiracy_theory "White genocide conspiracy theory") is a [white nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_nationalism "White nationalism") notion that [immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration "Immigration"), [integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_integration "Racial integration"), low fertility rates and abortion are being promoted in predominantly white countries to dispossess or eliminate [white people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_people "White people").[[201]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-201)[[202]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-KivistoRundblad-202)[[203]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-203)[[204]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-204)[[205]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-205)[[206]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-206) A 2017 study in France by [IFOP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_fran%C3%A7ais_d%27opinion_publique "Institut français d'opinion publique"), for example, found that 48% of participants believed that political and media elites are conspiring to replace white people with immigrants.[[207]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-207)
While the idea of [black genocide in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_genocide_in_the_United_States "Black genocide in the United States") is not a conspiracy theory, accusations that [birth control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_control_in_the_United_States "Birth control in the United States") and [medical abortions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_abortion "Medical abortion") are part of a deliberate and ongoing genocide of African Americans has been cited as a conspiracy theory.[[208]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-208)[[209]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-209)[[210]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-210)
"[The Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Plan_\(Washington,_D.C.\) "The Plan \(Washington, D.C.\)")" is an alleged plot by white power brokers in [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D.C. "Washington, D.C."), to take control of the city's local government from African Americans, who were a majority of the city's population from the late 1950s to the early 2010s and remain its largest ethnic group.[[211]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-211)[[212]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-212)
Numerous conspiracy theories surround the desire by followers of a [fandom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fandom "Fandom") for two [celebrities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebrities "Celebrities") to be in a [romantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_relationship "Romantic relationship") and/or [sexual relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_relationship "Sexual relationship"), known as [shipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipping_\(fandom\) "Shipping \(fandom\)").[[213]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-VoxAug2016-213) Many real-person shipping conspiracy theories involve claims that the pregnancies and children of partnered or married celebrity couples are fake.[[214]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-HOW_A_FAKE_BABY_IS_BORN-214) Proponents of celebrity shipping conspiracies that ship two celebrities of the same gender typically argue that they are being pro-LGBT by supporting two people who are forcibly closeted by a homophobic industry.[[215]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-215) Conspiracy communities about celebrity relationships tend to be created and dominated by women.[[214]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-HOW_A_FAKE_BABY_IS_BORN-214)
### Gaylor
Main article: [Gaylor conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaylor_conspiracy_theory "Gaylor conspiracy theory")
Gaylor is the [fringe theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fringe_theory "Fringe theory") that claims the American musician [Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift") to be gay or otherwise non-heterosexual.[[216]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-216)
### Larries
Main article: [Larries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larries "Larries")
Larries are a group of shipping conspiracy theorist fans, centered around the idea that two members of the boy band [One Direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Direction "One Direction"), [Harry Styles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Styles "Harry Styles") and [Louis Tomlinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Tomlinson "Louis Tomlinson"), are secretly a couple.[[217]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Tiffany_2022_173%E2%80%93222-217) These conspiracy theorists falsely claim that Styles and Tomlinson have been closeted by their management since the inception of the band, despite multiple claims otherwise by Styles, Tomlinson, their friends, and their family.[[217]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Tiffany_2022_173%E2%80%93222-217)
See also: [List of political conspiracies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_conspiracies "List of political conspiracies")
In the modern era, political conspiracy theories are often spread using [fake news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fake_news "Fake news") on social media. A 2017 study of fake news, which was published by the [Shorenstein Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shorenstein_Center "Shorenstein Center"), found that "[misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation "Misinformation") is currently predominantly a pathology of [the right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_politics "Right-wing politics")".[[218]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-218) Political conspiracy theories may take generalized and wide-ranging forms concerning wars and international bodies, but may also be seen at a localized level, such as the conspiracy theory pertaining to the [118th Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/118th_\(North_Waterloo\)_Battalion,_CEF "118th \(North Waterloo\) Battalion, CEF"), a British regiment stationed in [Kitchener, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitchener,_Ontario "Kitchener, Ontario"), during [World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_I "World War I"), which was believed by some in Kitchener to still be present years after the war ended and to be controlling local politics.[[219]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-219)
### BlueAnon
[BlueAnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BlueAnon "BlueAnon") is a set of loosely related left-wing conspiracy theories that suggest Donald Trump is engaged in elaborate plots to capture or maintain control of the United States Government.[[220]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-cjr-220)[[221]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-221)[[222]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-wired-222)
### Crisis actors
Further information: ["Crisis actor" as a conspiracy theory motif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_actor#Conspiracy_theories_and_defamation "Crisis actor")
[Crisis actors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_actors "Crisis actors") are individuals who portray disaster victims in [first responder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_responder "First responder") training exercises. Conspiracy theories allege that [mass shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_shootings "Mass shootings") and similar traumatic events are actually staged, with victims and their families being portrayed by covert crisis actors. 
### Illuminati in Europe
Conspiracy theories concerning the [Illuminati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illuminati "Illuminati"), a short-lived 18th-century [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment "Age of Enlightenment")-era [secret society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secret_society "Secret society"), appear to have originated in the late 19th century, when some Catholic conservatives in Europe came to believe that the group had been responsible for the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution "French Revolution") of 1789–1799.[[223]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-223) Hoaxes about the Illuminati were later spread in the 1960s by a group of American practical jokers known as the [Discordians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discordianism "Discordianism") who wrote a series of fake letters about the Illuminati to _[Playboy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playboy "Playboy")_.[[224]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-224)
### False flag operations
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/World_Trade_Center%2C_New_York_City_-_aerial_view_%28March_2001%29.jpg/250px-World_Trade_Center%2C_New_York_City_-_aerial_view_%28March_2001%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:World_Trade_Center,_New_York_City_-_aerial_view_\(March_2001\).jpg)The [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\) "World Trade Center \(1973–2001\)") towers prior to [9/11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11 "9/11")
[False flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_flag "False flag") operations are covert operations designed to appear as if they are being carried out by other entities. Some allegations of false flag operations have been verified or are subjects of legitimate historical dispute (such as the 1933 [Reichstag arson attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reichstag_fire "Reichstag fire")).[[225]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-225) Unsubstantiated allegations of such operations feature strongly in conspiracy theories. 
Such allegations have attached to [the bombing of Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Harbor_advance-knowledge_conspiracy_theory "Pearl Harbor advance-knowledge conspiracy theory"), [the Oklahoma City bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oklahoma_City_bombing_conspiracy_theories "Oklahoma City bombing conspiracy theories"), [the 2004 Madrid train bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Madrid_train_bombings_conspiracy_theories "2004 Madrid train bombings conspiracy theories"),[[226]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-226) the 1964 [Gulf of Tonkin incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Tonkin_incident "Gulf of Tonkin incident"),[[227]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-227) and the [Euromaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan "Euromaidan") massacre.[[228]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Young_2023_f202-228) The rise of [ISIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISIS "ISIS") gave rise to conspiracy theories that it had been created by the US, CIA, Mossad, or Hillary Clinton.[[229]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-229)[[230]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-230) The same happened after the rise of [Boko Haram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boko_Haram "Boko Haram").[[231]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-231)[[232]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-232)
Main article: [9/11 conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_conspiracy_theories "9/11 conspiracy theories")
See also: [9/11 Truth movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Truth_movement "9/11 Truth movement") and [9/11 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission "9/11 Commission")
The [attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks") on the US by [terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism "Terrorism") using hijacked aircraft on 11 September 2001 have proved [attractive to conspiracy theorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_conspiracy_theories "9/11 conspiracy theories"). Theories may include reference to missile or hologram technology. The most common theory is that the [Twin Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Towers_\(New_York_City\) "Twin Towers \(New York City\)") collapsed in controlled demolitions,[[233]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-233)[[234]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-234) a theory rejected by the engineering profession[[235]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-235) and the [9/11 Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission "9/11 Commission"). 
### Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting
Main article: [Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting_conspiracy_theories "Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting conspiracy theories")
A [2012 fatal mass shooting at Sandy Hook Elementary School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting "Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting") in Newtown, Connecticut, [prompted numerous conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting_conspiracy_theories "Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting conspiracy theories"), among them the claim that it was staged to promote gun control. Former Ku Klux Klan leader [David Duke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Duke "David Duke") blamed Zionist deception.[[236]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-236) Theorists such as [Alex Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Jones "Alex Jones") have suggested that the event was staged with actors.[[237]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-237)[[238]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-SnopesDebunk-238) Harassment of the bereaved families by conspiracy theorists [has resulted in actions for defamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Hook_Elementary_School_shooting_conspiracy_theories#Harassment_by_conspiracy_theorists "Sandy Hook Elementary School shooting conspiracy theories"). [Rush Limbaugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rush_Limbaugh "Rush Limbaugh") stated that the [Mayan Calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_phenomenon "2012 phenomenon") phenomenon drove the shooter [Adam Lanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Lanza "Adam Lanza").[[239]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-239)
### Clintons
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Bill_and_Hillary_%2801%29.jpg/250px-Bill_and_Hillary_%2801%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bill_and_Hillary_\(01\).jpg)[Bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton") and [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton"), main people supposedly involved in this conspiracy
The [Clinton body count conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_body_count_conspiracy_theory "Clinton body count conspiracy theory"), notably advanced by [Newsmax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsmax "Newsmax") publisher [Christopher Ruddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Ruddy "Christopher Ruddy"), asserts that US president [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton") and his wife [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton") have assassinated fifty or more of their associates and enemies.[[240]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-240)[[241]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-241) Such accusations have been around at least since the 1990s, when a pseudo-documentary film called _[The Clinton Chronicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Clinton_Chronicles "The Clinton Chronicles")_ , produced by [Larry Nichols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Nichols "Larry Nichols") and promoted by [Rev. Jerry Falwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Falwell "Jerry Falwell"), accused Bill Clinton of multiple crimes including murder.[[242]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-NYT-242)[[243]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-243)
### Jeffrey Epstein death conspiracy theories
See also: [Jeffrey Epstein death conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein_death_conspiracy_theories "Jeffrey Epstein death conspiracy theories")
The 2019 [death of Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Jeffrey_Epstein "Death of Jeffrey Epstein"), an American financier billionaire and convicted sex offender with ties to Bill Clinton, Donald Trump, and other members of the elite, has become the subject of conspiracy theories.[[244]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-244)[[245]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-245)
### 2017 Las Vegas shooting conspiracy theories
Main article: [2017 Las Vegas shooting conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Las_Vegas_shooting_conspiracy_theories "2017 Las Vegas shooting conspiracy theories")
The [2017 Las Vegas shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Las_Vegas_shooting "2017 Las Vegas shooting"), the deadliest [mass shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_shootings_in_the_United_States "Mass shootings in the United States") in modern U.S. history, has sparked numerous conspiracy theories. Some theorists suggest the use of a [machine gun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_gun "Machine gun") rather than [semi-automatic weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-automatic_firearm "Semi-automatic firearm"), claiming the rapid fire heard in videos indicates automatic gunfire. Others allege [government involvement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States "Federal government of the United States"), speculating that the incident was orchestrated to justify banning [bump stocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bump_stock "Bump stock"), devices that enable semi-automatic rifles to fire more rapidly. Additionally, there are theories about multiple shooters, fueled by eyewitness accounts and video evidence that purportedly show gunfire from different locations. Despite extensive investigations debunking these claims, they continue to circulate in various conspiracy theory communities. 
### FEMA
Further information: [FEMA camps conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FEMA_camps_conspiracy_theory "FEMA camps conspiracy theory")
The United States's [Federal Emergency Management Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Emergency_Management_Agency "Federal Emergency Management Agency") is the subject of many theories, including that the organization has been building concentration camps in the US to prepare for imposing martial law and genocide.[[246]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-246)
### African National Congress
Members of South Africa's [African National Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_National_Congress "African National Congress") party have long propagated conspiracy theories, frequently concerning the CIA and alleged white supremacists. In 2014, Deputy Minister of Defence [Kebby Maphatsoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kebby_Maphatsoe "Kebby Maphatsoe") joined others in accusing without evidence Public Protector [Thuli Madonsela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thuli_Madonsela "Thuli Madonsela") of being a US agent working to create a puppet government in South Africa.[[247]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-247)[[248]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-248)[[249]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-249)
### Barack Obama
See also: [Spygate (conspiracy theory)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spygate_\(conspiracy_theory\) "Spygate \(conspiracy theory\)"), [False or misleading statements by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_or_misleading_statements_by_Donald_Trump "False or misleading statements by Donald Trump"), [List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories_promoted_by_Donald_Trump "List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump"), and [Barack Obama citizenship conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_citizenship_conspiracy_theories "Barack Obama citizenship conspiracy theories")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Donald_Trump_%285440995138%29.jpg/250px-Donald_Trump_%285440995138%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Donald_Trump_\(5440995138\).jpg)[Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") has been a proponent of the conspiracy theory that [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") was not born in the United States.
[Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") has been the subject of numerous conspiracy theories. His presidency was the subject of a 2009 film, _The Obama Deception_ , by Alex Jones, which alleged that Obama's administration was a [puppet government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_government "Puppet government") for a wealthy elite. The ["birther"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_citizenship_conspiracy_theories "Barack Obama citizenship conspiracy theories") theory, which came to prominence in 2009, denies the legitimacy of Obama's presidency by claiming that he was not born in the US.[[250]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-250) This theory has persisted despite his [Hawaiian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaii "Hawaii") birth certificate and birth announcements in two Hawaiian newspapers in 1961.[[251]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-251) Notable promoters of the theory are dentist-lawyer [Orly Taitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orly_Taitz "Orly Taitz")[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Financial_Times-24) and President Donald Trump, who has since publicly acknowledged its falsity but is said to continue to advocate for it privately.[[252]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-252)[[253]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-253)[[254]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-254) Other theories claim that Obama, a Protestant Christian, [is secretly a Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_religion_conspiracy_theories "Barack Obama religion conspiracy theories"). 
[A pair of fatal attacks on US government facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Benghazi_attack "2012 Benghazi attack") in [Benghazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benghazi "Benghazi"), [Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libya "Libya"), by [Islamist terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_terrorism "Islamic terrorism") in 2012 has spawned numerous conspiracy theories, including allegations that Obama's administration arranged the attack for political reasons, and [Senator Rand Paul's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul "Rand Paul") claimed that the government's response to the incident was designed to distract from a secret CIA operation.[[255]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-255)[[256]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-256)[[257]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-257)
### Cultural Marxism
Further information: [Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Marxism_conspiracy_theory "Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory")
The intellectual group known as the [Frankfurt School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_School "Frankfurt School"), which emerged in the 1930s, has increasingly been [accused](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Marxism_conspiracy_theory "Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory") of promoting [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism "Communism") in capitalist societies. The term "Cultural Marxism" has been notably employed by conservative American movements such as the [Tea Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement "Tea Party movement"),[[258]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-258)[[259]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Berkowitz-259) and by Norwegian mass murderer [Anders Behring Breivik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Behring_Breivik "Anders Behring Breivik").[[260]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-260)
Further information: [Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Marxism_conspiracy_theory "Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory")
"Woke" conspiracy theories allege that progressive social justice movements are part of a coordinated, elite agenda—often linked to the "Cultural Marxism" conspiracy theory—to dismantle Western culture, capitalism, and traditional values.[[261]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-261) These claims typically portray movements for racial equity, LGBTQ+ rights, and feminism as engineered efforts to promote discrimination against white people and Christians.[[262]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-262)
Scholars note this ideology combines elements of anti-feminism, white supremacy, and anti-intellectualism to argue that a "purge" of progressive influence is needed to restore society.[[263]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-263) The conspiracy functions to identify and demonize an "Other" believed to be responsible for social and political failings.[[264]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-264)
The narrative frames "woke" ideology as a continuation of the Cultural Marxism conspiracy theory, which claims that Jewish intellectuals of the [Frankfurt School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurt_School "Frankfurt School") initiated a plan to destabilize Western civilization through control of education, media, and cultural institutions.[[265]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-265) This theory has been used to justify far-right terrorism, including by Anders Breivik.[[266]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-266)
These theories reject the existence of systemic racism and argue that diversity and inclusion initiatives constitute harmful "reverse discrimination" against white people.[[267]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-267) Scholars have documented how the term "[woke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woke "Woke")"—which originated in Black American communities to signify awareness of racial injustice—has been repurposed by conservative critics to describe an allegedly conspiratorial left-wing ideology.[[268]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-268)
Such theories are promoted by political figures, media personalities, and some academics to rally opposition against social change.[[269]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-269) Researchers have documented their spread across online platforms including Twitter, Telegram, and YouTube, where they function to disseminate far-right ideology.[[270]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-270)
### Deep state 
Further information: [Deep state in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_state_in_the_United_States "Deep state in the United States"), [List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories_promoted_by_Donald_Trump "List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump"), and [QAnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QAnon "QAnon")
While the term is occasionally used as a neutral term to denote a nation's bureaucracy,[[271]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-271) the conspiratorial notion of a "[deep state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_state "Deep state")" originated principally in Middle Eastern and North African politics with some basis in truth, and has [been known in the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_state_in_the_United_States "Deep state in the United States") since the 1960s. It was revived under the Trump presidency.[[272]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-272)[[273]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-273) "Deep state" in the latter sense refers to an unidentified insider "power elite" who manipulate a nation's politics and government. Proponents have included Canadian author [Peter Dale Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Dale_Scott "Peter Dale Scott"), who has promoted the idea in the US since the 1990s, as well as _[Breitbart News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breitbart_News "Breitbart News")_ , _[Infowars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infowars "Infowars")_ and [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump").[[274]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-274) A 2017 poll by [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\) "ABC News \(United States\)") and _The Washington Post_ indicated that 48% of Americans believe in the existence of a conspiratorial "deep state" in the US.[[275]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-275)[[276]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-276)
### Donald Trump assassination attempts
Main articles: [Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Donald_Trump_in_Pennsylvania "Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Pennsylvania") and [Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Donald_Trump_in_Florida "Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Florida")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Arrest_of_Ryan_Wesley_Routh_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Arrest_of_Ryan_Wesley_Routh_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Arrest_of_Ryan_Wesley_Routh_\(cropped\).jpg)[Ryan Wesley Routh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Wesley_Routh "Ryan Wesley Routh") being detained by police following his attempt to assassinate Trump.
[Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") survived two assassination attempts during his [2024 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign "Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign"). The first was during a speech in [Butler, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler,_Pennsylvania "Butler, Pennsylvania") by 20-year-old [Thomas Crooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Crooks "Thomas Crooks") on July 13, 2024, and the second occurred while he was golfing at [Trump International Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_International_Golf_Club_\(West_Palm_Beach\) "Trump International Golf Club \(West Palm Beach\)") in [West Palm Beach, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Palm_Beach,_Florida "West Palm Beach, Florida") by 58-year-old activist [Ryan Wesley Routh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Wesley_Routh "Ryan Wesley Routh") on September 15, 2024. 
The lack of security at the Butler, Pennsylvania rally and of public information about Crooks has spawned [conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories "Conspiracy theories").[[277]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Spring-2024-277)[[278]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Frenkel-2024-278)[[279]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Chidi-2024-279)[[280]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Seven-2024-280) Public opinion polls show that over half of Americans believe that Crooks did not act alone.[[281]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Polls-2024-281) Some of the theories claim either that the incident was planned by incumbent President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") to prevent Trump from getting elected,[[282]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-282) that it was staged by Republicans to improve Trump's image,[[283]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-283)[[284]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-284)[[285]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-285) or that it was planned by the FBI.[[286]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Fallonconspiracy-286) Several conspiracy theorists including [Tucker Carlson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucker_Carlson "Tucker Carlson") have claimed that Crooks was a federal asset.[[287]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Who_is_Thomas_Crooks-287)
The fact that Trump's second assassination attempt in West Palm Beach, Florida, occurred only two months after his first has also spawned conspiracy theories. Some theories argued the event was also staged, or that the Secret Service intentionally allowed Routh to get within range of the golf course. Due to Routh's public support for Ukraine, including his efforts to recruit foreign fighters, some speculated he was an intelligence operative.[[288]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-288)[[289]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-289)
### Sutherland Springs
See also: [Sutherland Springs church shooting § Hoaxes and conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutherland_Springs_church_shooting#Hoaxes_and_conspiracy_theories "Sutherland Springs church shooting")
The 2017 [Sutherland Springs church shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sutherland_Springs_church_shooting "Sutherland Springs church shooting") has also been the subject of multiple conspiracy theories. The shooter has been linked to multiple conspiracies, such as identifying him as a Democrat, Hillary Clinton supporter, [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders "Bernie Sanders") supporter, "[alt-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alt-left "Alt-left")" supporter, [antifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antifa_\(United_States\) "Antifa \(United States\)") member, or [radical Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism "Islamism");[[290]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-290)[[291]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-ansari-291) or claiming that he carried an antifa flag and told churchgoers: "This is a communist revolution".[[292]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Snopes.com-292) Some reports also falsely claimed that he targeted the church because they were white conservatives.[[293]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-griffin-293)
Main article: [Conspiracy theories related to the Trump–Ukraine scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories_related_to_the_Trump%E2%80%93Ukraine_scandal "Conspiracy theories related to the Trump–Ukraine scandal")
Beginning in 2017, a sprawling conspiracy theory emerged from [4chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4chan "4chan") and was spread via right-wing message boards and websites, then via Breitbart and [Fox News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News "Fox News") to then-President Donald Trump and his allies. The conspiracy theory holds both that Ukraine (rather than Russia) had interfered in the 2016 United States elections, and that then-Vice President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") had intervened to protect a company in which his son Hunter was involved. _[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker "The New Yorker")_ found that reporting of the conspiracy in the right wing media was initiated by [Peter Schweizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Schweizer "Peter Schweizer"), a former _[Breitbart News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breitbart_News "Breitbart News")_ contributor and president of The [Government Accountability Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Accountability_Institute "Government Accountability Institute"), "a self-styled corruption watchdog group chaired and funded by conservative mega-donor [Rebekah Mercer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebekah_Mercer "Rebekah Mercer")"[[294]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-294) and founded by [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon "Steve Bannon").[[295]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-295)
Main article: [Biden–Ukraine conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden%E2%80%93Ukraine_conspiracy_theory "Biden–Ukraine conspiracy theory")
A series of allegations have been made that [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") and his son [Hunter Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Biden "Hunter Biden") coordinated efforts against anti-corruption investigations in [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine") into the Ukrainian gas company [Burisma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burisma "Burisma"), on whose Board Hunter Biden sat.[[296]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-296) A joint investigation by two Republican Senate committees released in September 2020 found no evidence of wrongdoing by Joe Biden; a subsequent report in August 2024 asserted Biden had "engaged in impeachable conduct", but did not recommend impeachment. 
### Michelle Obama
Some conservatives have supported a conspiracy theory falsely claiming that former [FLOTUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Lady_of_the_United_States "First Lady of the United States"), [Michelle Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Obama "Michelle Obama"), is secretly [transgender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender "Transgender"), and that her [name was originally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadnaming "Deadnaming") "Michael".[[297]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-297)[[298]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-298)[[299]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-299) [Alex Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Jones "Alex Jones") has been a vocal supporter of the theory,[[300]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-300)[[301]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-301) initially promoting it in 2014 after [Joan Rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_Rivers "Joan Rivers") made a joke referring to Obama as transgender.[[302]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-302)[[303]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-303)
Further information: [Tanzimat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzimat "Tanzimat"), [Ottoman Reform Edict of 1856](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Reform_Edict_of_1856 "Ottoman Reform Edict of 1856"), [Young Ottomans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Ottomans "Young Ottomans"), [Young Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Turks "Young Turks"), and [Atatürk's reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk%27s_reforms "Atatürk's reforms")
See also: [War against Islam conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_against_Islam_conspiracy_theory "War against Islam conspiracy theory")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Turks_War_on_Patriarchs%2C_following_the_Abolition_of_the_Caliphate_as_reported_in_The_New_York_Times%2C_16_March_1924.png/250px-Turks_War_on_Patriarchs%2C_following_the_Abolition_of_the_Caliphate_as_reported_in_The_New_York_Times%2C_16_March_1924.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Turks_War_on_Patriarchs,_following_the_Abolition_of_the_Caliphate_as_reported_in_The_New_York_Times,_16_March_1924.png)His [abolition of the caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolition_of_the_Caliphate "Abolition of the Caliphate") and many other reforms that secularized Turkey made [Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Atat%C3%BCrk "Mustafa Kemal Atatürk") the target of [Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism "Islamism") conspiracy theories.
The belief that the [modernist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernity "Modernity") and [secularist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism "Secularism") movements and reforms in the [Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Empire "Ottoman Empire") and the [Turkish Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey") were [Judeo-Masonic conspiracies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Masonic_conspiracy_theory "Judeo-Masonic conspiracy theory") is dominant among [Islamists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism "Islamism"). Because of their adherence to [traditionalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_conservatism "Traditionalist conservatism") elements and their creation of [cults of personality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult_of_personality "Cult of personality") for the Ottoman [sultans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sultans_of_the_Ottoman_Empire "List of sultans of the Ottoman Empire"), Islamists portray anything contrary to Islamic tradition as evil. According to them, the [constitutionalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionalism "Constitutionalism") movement ended absolute monarchy, the end of traditional religious institutions, and the introduction of a [secular state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state "Secular state") were the work of Jews, Freemasons, or _[Dönmes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%B6nmeh "Dönmeh")_. For example, according to their conspiracy theory, Turkish president [Mustafa Kemal Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Kemal_Atat%C3%BCrk "Mustafa Kemal Atatürk") was a _Dönme_.[[304]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaer2013523%E2%80%93555-304)
Various conspiracy theories against Atatürk have been put forward by [Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamism "Islamism") circles who did not support his [secularist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism "Secularism") [reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk%27s_reforms "Atatürk's reforms"). [Kadir Mısıroğlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadir_M%C4%B1s%C4%B1ro%C4%9Flu "Kadir Mısıroğlu"), known for his hatred of Atatürk, is behind many conspiracy theories about Atatürk.[[305]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Yalansavar-305)[[306]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-306) One of these conspiracy theories is that [Anatolia was invaded by the Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greco-Turkish_War_\(1919%E2%80%931922\) "Greco-Turkish War \(1919–1922\)") as a result of Atatürk's agreement with the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland "United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland") to overthrow the [caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Caliphate "Ottoman Caliphate").[[305]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Yalansavar-305)[[307]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-307) Another conspiracy theory about Atatürk is that he was poisoned to death by the [Freemasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freemasonry "Freemasonry") due to the closure of [Masonic lodges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masonic_lodge "Masonic lodge")[[308]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-308) in Turkey in 1935. In 2015, _[Yeni Şafak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeni_%C5%9Eafak "Yeni Şafak")_ claimed that [İsmet İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC "İsmet İnönü") was in charge of planning the murder.[[64]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-yenisafak-64)
In 2007, the bestselling book in Turkey was _[Musa'nın Çocukları: Tayyip ve Emine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Children_of_Moses "The Children of Moses")_ (The Children of Moses: Tayyip and Emine) by [Ergün Poyraz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erg%C3%BCn_Poyraz "Ergün Poyraz"). Poyraz claims that there is an [international Jewish conspiracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Jewish_conspiracy "International Jewish conspiracy") pulling the strings behind the world, including installing [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan "Recep Tayyip Erdoğan") as prime minister of Turkey.[[309]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaer2013523-309)
### Golden billion threatens Russia
The [golden billion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_billion "Golden billion") is an idea there is an anti-Russian Western population of approximately one billion seeking to appropriate Russia's natural resources. It is a justification for Russian leader [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin")'s imperial aggression, claiming to be a defense against a far-reaching covert attack.[[310]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-310) The theory was first advanced under the name A. Kuzmich.[[311]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-311)
### Voting pencils
Main article: [Voting pencil conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_pencil_conspiracy_theory "Voting pencil conspiracy theory")
The [voting pencil conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_pencil_conspiracy_theory "Voting pencil conspiracy theory") concerns pencils at [polling places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polling_place "Polling place") enabling [electoral fraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_fraud "Electoral fraud") through erasing legitimate votes. It has been nicknamed "Pencilgate" and promoted on [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter") (now X) as "#Pencilgate" and "#UsePens". The conspiracy theory originated in the United Kingdom and was originally promoted by "Yes" voters in the unsuccessful [2014 Scottish independence referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Scottish_independence_referendum "2014 Scottish independence referendum"), with supporters of [Scottish independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_independence "Scottish independence") (who usually lean to the [left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_politics "Left-wing politics") of the [political spectrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_spectrum "Political spectrum")) claiming that electoral fraud was the reason the referendum failed. However, the theory was later adopted by supporters of the "Leave" vote in the successful [2016 Brexit referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_Kingdom_European_Union_membership_referendum "2016 United Kingdom European Union membership referendum") (who usually lean to the [right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_politics "Right-wing politics") of the political spectrum).[[312]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-312) The conspiracy theory has since spread to Australia, with right-wing politician [Pauline Hanson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauline_Hanson "Pauline Hanson"), a [Queensland Senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Senators_from_Queensland "List of Senators from Queensland") who is the founder and leader of the [One Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pauline_Hanson%27s_One_Nation "Pauline Hanson's One Nation") party, promoted the conspiracy theory in the lead-up to the [2022 Australian federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_federal_election "2022 Australian federal election").[[313]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-313)
While pencils are provided at polling places in the United Kingdom and Australia, in neither country is it required that they be used.[[314]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-314) On their website, the [Australian Electoral Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Electoral_Commission "Australian Electoral Commission") states: 
> While the provision of pencils used to be a legal requirement, since 2020 under Section 206 of the _[Commonwealth Electoral Act 1918](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Electoral_Act_1918 "Commonwealth Electoral Act 1918")_ , the AEC is required to provide an "implement or method for voters to mark their ballot papers". 
> The AEC has found from experience that pencils are the most reliable implements for marking ballot papers. Pencils are practical because they don't run out and the polling staff check and sharpen pencils as necessary throughout election day. Pencils can be stored between elections and they work better in tropical areas. 
> There is, however, nothing to prevent an elector from marking their ballot paper with a pen if they so wish.[[315]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-315)
While the AEC only conducts federal elections, voters can use either pencil or pen in state, territory and local elections. Pencils are supplied at polling places for federal elections and for state elections in [Tasmania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasmania "Tasmania")[[316]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-316) and [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(state\) "Victoria \(state\)"),[[317]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-317) while pens are supplied at polling places for state elections in [New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales "New South Wales")[[318]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-318) and [Western Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Australia "Western Australia"). In 2015, the [New South Wales Electoral Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales_Electoral_Commission "New South Wales Electoral Commission") made the decision to replace pencils with pens at polling places due to controversies regarding their usage and at the [2015 state election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_New_South_Wales_state_election "2015 New South Wales state election"), pens began being supplied at polling places across the state.[[319]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-319) Similarly, the [Western Australian Electoral Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Australian_Electoral_Commission "Western Australian Electoral Commission") has provided pens instead of pencils at polling places since [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Western_Australian_state_election "2017 Western Australian state election").[[320]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-320)
### QAnon
Main article: [QAnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QAnon "QAnon")
QAnon is a [far-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right "Far-right") [American political conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_political_conspiracy_theory "American political conspiracy theory") and [political movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_movement "Political movement") that originated in 2017.[[321]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-nymag-321) QAnon centers on fabricated claims made by an anonymous individual or individuals known as "Q". Those claims have been relayed and developed by [online communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_community "Virtual community") and [influencers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Influencers "Influencers"). Their core belief is that a [cabal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabal "Cabal") of [Satanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theistic_Satanism "Theistic Satanism"),[[322]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Bracewell-322)[[323]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Crossley-323) [cannibalistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_cannibalism "Human cannibalism") [child molesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_molesters "Child molesters") are operating a global child [sex trafficking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_trafficking "Sex trafficking") ring that conspired against [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump").[[327]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-327) QAnon has direct roots in [Pizzagate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pizzagate_conspiracy_theory "Pizzagate conspiracy theory"), an Internet conspiracy theory that appeared one year earlier, but also incorporates elements of many other theories.[[328]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Bigtent-328) QAnon has been described as a [cult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult "Cult").[[328]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Bigtent-328)[[329]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-cult-329)
### State-sponsored kidnappings of Muslims in Sweden
Main article: [Alleged state-sponsored kidnappings of Muslims in Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alleged_state-sponsored_kidnappings_of_Muslims_in_Sweden "Alleged state-sponsored kidnappings of Muslims in Sweden")
Since December 2021, disinformation has been spread concerning [Swedish social services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_in_Sweden "Welfare in Sweden") taking [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslims "Muslims") children into care without a legal basis.[[330]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-330) As a result of the conspiracy theory, two Swedish people were killed in [Brussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels "Brussels") in 2023.[[331]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-331)[[332]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-332)
### Sustainable development
Various theories have emerged in response to concepts or international agreements relating to [sustainable development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_development "Sustainable development"). These include theories that [Agenda 21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agenda_21 "Agenda 21"), a non-binding action plan of the United Nations, is a plot, disguised as an environmental movement, to end individual freedom and establish a one-world government[[333]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-SPLC_20120401-333)[[334]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Hinkes-Jones_20120829-334) to cut the world population by 85%,[[333]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-SPLC_20120401-333) and/or to introduce surveillance by the 5G network.[[335]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-APT:_Conspiracy_Theories:_A_Guide-335)
Similarly, the [Great Reset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Reset "Great Reset") Initiative is an economic recovery plan drawn up by the World Economic Forum (WEF) in response to the COVID-19 pandemic that prioritizes [sustainable development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_development "Sustainable development").[[336]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-guardian060320-336) It has been the basis for theories that the COVID-19 pandemic will be used, or was even created by a secret group in order to seize control of the global economy.[[337]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-BBC20201122-337)
The [urban planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_planning "Urban planning") concept of [15-minute cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15-minute_cities "15-minute cities") envisages that all of life's necessities (e.g. work, shops, schools, medical centres) should be within a short walk or bike ride from people's homes.[[335]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-APT:_Conspiracy_Theories:_A_Guide-335) This would thereby encourage people to use their cars less and reduce emissions and pollution in neighbourhoods. However, it has been interpreted as a plan to restrict freedom of movement outside of the 15-minute neighbourhood.[[335]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-APT:_Conspiracy_Theories:_A_Guide-335) These theories occasionally overlap with [anti-vaccine misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-vaccine_misinformation "Anti-vaccine misinformation"), and fears of a [world government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_government "World government").[[338]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-338)
