### Alternative therapy suppression
A 2013 study approved by the [University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago "University of Chicago") suggested that almost half of Americans believe at least one medical conspiracy theory, with 37% believing that the [Food and Drug Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Drug_Administration "Food and Drug Administration") deliberately suppresses 'natural' cures due to influence from the pharmaceutical industry.[[339]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-339) A prominent proponent of comparable conspiracy theories has been convicted fraudster [Kevin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Trudeau "Kevin Trudeau").[[340]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Scientific_American_2006-340)
### Artificial diseases
See also: [HIV/AIDS denialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS_denialism "HIV/AIDS denialism") and [Misinformation related to the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation_related_to_the_COVID-19_pandemic "Misinformation related to the COVID-19 pandemic")
Scientists have found evidence that [HIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV "HIV") was transferred from monkeys to humans in the 1930s. Evidence exists, however, that the KGB deliberately disseminated a notion in the 1980s that [it was invented by the CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discredited_HIV/AIDS_origins_theories "Discredited HIV/AIDS origins theories").[[341]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-theweek.co.uk-341) This idea, and similar ideas concerning [Ebola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebola "Ebola"), have since been promoted by persons such as actor [Steven Seagal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Seagal "Steven Seagal"),[[342]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-342)[[343]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-time1-343)[[344]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-abc1-344) [Nation of Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation_of_Islam "Nation of Islam") leader [Louis Farrakhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Farrakhan "Louis Farrakhan") and former South Africa President [Thabo Mbeki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thabo_Mbeki "Thabo Mbeki").[[341]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-theweek.co.uk-341)[[345]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-horowitz-345)[[346]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-fox1-346) Similar conspiracy theories allege that pharmaceutical companies assist in the creation of conditions and diseases including [ADHD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ADHD "ADHD"), [HSV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herpes_simplex_virus "Herpes simplex virus") and [HPV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HPV "HPV"). 
### COVID-19 pandemic
Main article: [Misinformation related to the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misinformation_related_to_the_COVID-19_pandemic "Misinformation related to the COVID-19 pandemic")
A number of conspiracy theories have been promoted about the origin and purported motive behind the [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2 "SARS-CoV-2") virus and [its spread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic").[[6]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Andrade2020-6) Some claimed that the virus was engineered,[[347]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-347) that it may have been a Chinese or United States bioweapon,[[6]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Andrade2020-6)[[348]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-20200129washingtonpost-348)[[349]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-20200208economist-349)[[350]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-20200326propublica-350) a Jewish plot, part of which is to force mass vaccinations or sterilizations,[[351]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-jpost621393-351)[[352]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-352)[[353]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-353) spread as part of a Muslim conspiracy,[[354]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-20200405theguardian-354)[[355]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-355) a population control scheme,[[356]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-bbc_misinfo-356)[[357]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-20200123buzzfeednews-357) or related to 5G mobile phone networks.[[358]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-bbc-5g-358)[[359]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-guardian-vodafone-exec-5g-coronavirus-conspiracy-theory-video-revealed-pastor-luton-jonathon-james-359)
### Fluoridation
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Dr._Strangelove_-_Ripper_and_Mandrake.png/330px-Dr._Strangelove_-_Ripper_and_Mandrake.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dr._Strangelove_-_Ripper_and_Mandrake.png)Ripper explains to Mandrake that he discovered the communist plot to pollute Americans' "precious bodily fluids", a reference to the John Birch Society's anti-fluoridation conspiracy theories (_[Dr. Strangelove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Strangelove "Dr. Strangelove")_ , 1964).
[Water fluoridation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_fluoridation "Water fluoridation") is the controlled addition of [fluoride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fluoride "Fluoride") to a public water supply to reduce tooth decay.[[360]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-360) Although many dental-health organizations support it, some conspiracy theorists[[361]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Armfield-361) claim that it was a way to dispose of industrial waste,[[362]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Fluoraphobia-362)[[363]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-363) or that it exists to obscure a failure to provide dental care to the poor.[[361]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Armfield-361) A further theory promoted by the [John Birch Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Birch_Society "John Birch Society") in the 1960s described fluoridation as a [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist "Communist") plot to weaken the American population.[[364]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-364)
### Vaccination
Further information: [False or misleading statements by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_or_misleading_statements_by_Donald_Trump "False or misleading statements by Donald Trump"), [List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories_promoted_by_Donald_Trump "List of conspiracy theories promoted by Donald Trump"), and [Vaccine misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaccine_misinformation "Vaccine misinformation")
It is claimed that the pharmaceutical industry has mounted a cover-up of [a causal link between vaccines and autism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MMR_vaccine_controversy "MMR vaccine controversy"). The conspiracy theory developed after the publication in Britain in 1998 of a fraudulent paper by discredited former doctor [Andrew Wakefield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Wakefield "Andrew Wakefield").[[365]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-365) The resulting anti-vaccine movement has been promoted by a number of prominent persons including [Rob Schneider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rob_Schneider "Rob Schneider"),[[366]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-366) [Jim Carrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Carrey "Jim Carrey"),[[367]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-367) [US president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_president "US president") [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump")[[368]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-368)[[369]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-369) and [Robert F. Kennedy Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_F._Kennedy_Jr. "Robert F. Kennedy Jr."), Trump's pick to lead the US [Department of Health and Human Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Health_and_Human_Services "Department of Health and Human Services").[[370]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-mnookin-2017-370)[[371]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-NBC_2021-03-11-371)[[372]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Time20230614-372) It has led to increased rates of infection and death from diseases such as measles and COVID-19[[373]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-373) in many countries, including the US, Italy, Germany, Romania and the UK.[[374]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-374)[[375]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-375)[[376]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-376)[[377]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-377) Vaccine conspiracy theories have been widespread in [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria") since at least 2003, as well as in [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan "Pakistan"). Such theories may feature claims that vaccines are part of a secret anti-Islam plot, and have been linked to fatal mass shootings and bombings at vaccine clinics in both countries.[[378]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-378)[[379]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-379)[[380]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-380)
## Outer space
Scientific space programs are of particular interest to conspiracy theorists. The most prolific theories allege that [the US Moon landings were staged by NASA in a film studio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_landing_conspiracy_theories "Moon landing conspiracy theories"), with some alleging the involvement of director [Stanley Kubrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kubrick "Stanley Kubrick").[[381]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-381) The [Soviet space program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_space_program "Soviet space program") has also attracted theories that [the government concealed evidence of failed flights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lost_Cosmonauts "Lost Cosmonauts"). A more recent theory, emergent following the activities of [hacker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_hacker "Computer hacker") [Gary McKinnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_McKinnon "Gary McKinnon"),[[382]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-382) suggests that a secret program of crewed space fleets exists, supposedly acting under the United Nations.[[383]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-383)
Conspiracy theorists have long posited a plot by organizations such as NASA to conceal the existence of a large planet in the [Solar System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_System "Solar System") known as [Nibiru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nibiru_cataclysm "Nibiru cataclysm") or Planet X, which is alleged to pass close enough to the Earth to destroy it. Predictions for the date of destruction have included 2003, 2012 and 2017. The theory began to develop following the publication of _The 12th Planet_ (1976), by Russian-American author Zecharia Sitchin, was given its full form by [Nancy Lieder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Lieder "Nancy Lieder"), and has since been promoted by American conspiracy theorist and [end times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eschatology "Eschatology") theorist [David Meade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Meade_\(author\) "David Meade \(author\)").[[384]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-384) The notion received renewed attention during the period prior to the [solar eclipse of 21 August 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_eclipse_of_August_21,_2017 "Solar eclipse of August 21, 2017").[[385]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-385)[[386]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-386) Other conspiracy theorists in 2017 also predicted Nibiru would appear, including Terral Croft and [YouTube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube "YouTube") pastor Paul Begley.[[387]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-387)[[388]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-388)
### Extraterrestrials and UFOs
See also: [UFO conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UFO_conspiracy_theory "UFO conspiracy theory")
Among the foremost concerns of conspiracy theorists are questions of alien life; for example, allegations of government cover-ups of the supposed [Roswell incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roswell_incident "Roswell incident") or activity at [Area 51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_51 "Area 51").[[389]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-389) [Multiple reports of dead cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattle_mutilation "Cattle mutilation") found with absent body parts and seemingly drained of blood have emerged worldwide since at least the 1960s. This phenomenon has spawned theories variously concerning aliens and secret government or military experiments.[[390]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-390) Prominent among such theorists is [Linda Moulton Howe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Moulton_Howe "Linda Moulton Howe"), author of _Alien Harvest_ (1989).[[391]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-391)[[392]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Barkun2006-392)
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/David_Icke%2C_7_June_2013_%281%29%2C_cropped.jpg/250px-David_Icke%2C_7_June_2013_%281%29%2C_cropped.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:David_Icke,_7_June_2013_\(1\),_cropped.jpg)English conspiracy theorist [David Icke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Icke "David Icke")
Many conspiracy theories have drawn inspiration from the writings of [ancient astronaut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_astronaut "Ancient astronaut") proponent [Zecharia Sitchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zecharia_Sitchin "Zecharia Sitchin"),[[393]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Robertson-393) who declared that the [Anunnaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anunnaki "Anunnaki") from [Sumerian mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumerian_religion "Sumerian religion") were actually a race of extraterrestrial beings who came to Earth around 500,000 years ago in order to mine gold.[[393]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Robertson-393)[[394]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-394)[[395]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-395) In his 1994 book _Humanity's Extraterrestrial Origins: ET Influences on Humankind's Biological and Cultural Evolution_ , Arthur Horn proposed that the Anunnaki were a race of [blood-drinking, shape-shifting alien reptiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reptilian_humanoid "Reptilian humanoid").[[393]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Robertson-393) This theory was adapted and elaborated on by British conspiracy theorist [David Icke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Icke "David Icke"),[[393]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Robertson-393) who maintains that the [Bush family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bush_family "Bush family"), [Margaret Thatcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret_Thatcher "Margaret Thatcher"), [Bob Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hope "Bob Hope"), and the [British royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_royal_family "British royal family"), among others, are or were such creatures, or have been under their control.[[396]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-396) Icke's critics have suggested that 'reptilians' may be seen as an antisemitic code word, a charge he has denied.[[88]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-The_Week-88)
## Science and technology
### Climate change
Further information: [Climate change conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_conspiracy_theory "Climate change conspiracy theory")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/High_Frequency_Active_Auroral_Research_Program_site.jpg/250px-High_Frequency_Active_Auroral_Research_Program_site.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:High_Frequency_Active_Auroral_Research_Program_site.jpg)Aerial view of the HAARP site, [Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska "Alaska")
A [climate change conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_conspiracy_theory "Climate change conspiracy theory") typically alleges that the science behind [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change "Climate change") has been invented or distorted for ideological or financial reasons—higher taxation, controls on lifestyle, and more authoritarian government.[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-The_Daily_Telegraph-31) [US president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_president "US president") [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump"),[[397]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-397)[[398]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-398) US Senator [James Inhofe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Inhofe "James Inhofe"),[[399]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-theguardian.com-399) British journalist [Christopher Booker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Booker "Christopher Booker"),[[399]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-theguardian.com-399) and Viscount [Christopher Monckton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Monckton "Christopher Monckton")[[400]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-400) have promoted such theories. Popular author [Michael Crichton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Crichton "Michael Crichton") wrote a [novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Fear "State of Fear") based on this premise. 
### Weather and earthquake control projects
Numerous theories pertain to real or alleged weather-controlling projects. Theories include the debunked assertion that [HAARP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAARP "HAARP"), a radio-technology research program funded by the US government, is a secret weather-controlling system. Some theorists have blamed 2005's [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina "Hurricane Katrina") and 2024's [Helene and Milton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories_about_the_2024_Atlantic_hurricane_season "Conspiracy theories about the 2024 Atlantic hurricane season") on HAARP.[[401]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-HAARP_Katrina_Helene_Milton-401) HAARP has also been suggested to have somehow caused earthquakes, such as the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake "2010 Haiti earthquake"), the [2011 Tōhoku earthquake and tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_T%C5%8Dhoku_earthquake_and_tsunami "2011 Tōhoku earthquake and tsunami"), the [2013 Saravan earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Saravan_earthquake "2013 Saravan earthquake") or the [2023 Turkey–Syria earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Turkey%E2%80%93Syria_earthquakes "2023 Turkey–Syria earthquakes").[[402]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-402) Some HAARP-related claims refer to mind-control technology.[[403]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-403) Also, of interest to conspiracy theorists are [cloud-seeding technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloud_seeding "Cloud seeding"). These include a debunked allegation[[404]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-404) that the British military's [Project Cumulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Cumulus "Project Cumulus") caused the fatal 1952 [Lynmouth Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynmouth_Flood "Lynmouth Flood") in Devon, England,[[405]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-405) [California drought manipulation conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_drought_manipulation_conspiracy_theory "California drought manipulation conspiracy theory"), and claims concerning a secret project said to have caused the [2010 Pakistan floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Pakistan_floods "2010 Pakistan floods").[[406]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-406)
### MKUltra
[Genuine American research in the 1950s and 1960s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MKUltra "MKUltra") into chemical interrogation and mind-control techniques were followed by many conspiracy theories (like [Project Monarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Monarch "Project Monarch")), especially following CIA Director Richard Helm's 1973 order to destroy all files related to the project. These theories include the allegation that the mass fatality at [Jonestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonestown "Jonestown") in 1978 was connected to an MKUltra experiment.[[407]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-407)
### Flat Earth
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Flat_Earth_Society_Logo.png/120px-Flat_Earth_Society_Logo.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flat_Earth_Society_Logo.png)Logo of the Flat Earth Society, 2013
[Flat Earth theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_Earth_theory "Flat Earth theory") first emerged in 19th-century England, despite the Earth's spherical nature having been known since at least the time of [Pythagoras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythagoras "Pythagoras"). It has in recent years been promoted by American software consultant [Mark Sargent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Sargent_\(flat_Earth_proponent\) "Mark Sargent \(flat Earth proponent\)") through the use of [YouTube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube "YouTube") videos.[[408]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-408) Flat-earther conspiracy theorists hold that planet Earth is not a sphere, and that evidence has been faked or suppressed to hide the fact that it is instead a disc, or a single infinite [plane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plane_\(geometry\) "Plane \(geometry\)"). The conspiracy often implicates NASA. Other claims include that [GPS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPS "GPS") devices are rigged to make aircraft pilots wrongly believe they are flying around a globe.[[409]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-409)[[410]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-410)
### RFID chips
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Oyster_card_partially_destroyed.jpg/250px-Oyster_card_partially_destroyed.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Oyster_card_partially_destroyed.jpg)An RFID tag, exposed by the damage to this [Oyster card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyster_card "Oyster card")
[Radio frequency identification chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RFID "RFID") (RFID), such as those implanted into pets as a means of tracking, have drawn the interest of conspiracy theorists who posit that this technology is secretly widely [implanted in humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio-frequency_identification#Human_implantation "Radio-frequency identification"). Former [Whitby, England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitby "Whitby") town councilor Simon Parkes has promoted this theory, which may be related to conspiracy theories concerning vaccination, electronic banking and the Antichrist.[[411]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-411)[[412]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-412)
### Technology suppression
Numerous theories pertain to the alleged suppression of certain technologies and energies. Such theories may focus on the [Vril Society Conspiracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vril "Vril"), allegations of the suppression of the [electric car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_car "Electric car") by fossil-fuel companies (as detailed in the 2006 documentary _[Who Killed the Electric Car?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who_Killed_the_Electric_Car%3F "Who Killed the Electric Car?")_), and the [Phoebus cartel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebus_cartel "Phoebus cartel"), set up in 1924, which has been accused of suppressing longer-lasting light bulbs.[[413]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-413) Other long-standing allegations include the suppression of [perpetual motion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perpetual_motion "Perpetual motion") and [cold fusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_fusion "Cold fusion") technology by government agencies, special interest groups, or fraudulent inventors.[[414]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-autogenerated6-414) Promoters of alternative energy theories have included Thomas Henry Moray,[[415]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-415) [Eugene Mallove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_Mallove "Eugene Mallove"), and convicted American fraudster [Stanley Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Meyer "Stanley Meyer").[[416]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-416)
### Weaponry
Conspiracy theorists often attend to new military technologies, both real and imagined. Subjects of theories include: the alleged [Philadelphia Experiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Experiment "Philadelphia Experiment"), a supposed attempt to turn a US Navy warship invisible;[[417]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-417) the alleged [Montauk Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montauk_Project "Montauk Project"), a supposed government program to learn about mind control and time travel; and the so-called "[tsunami bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsunami_bomb "Tsunami bomb")", which is alleged to have caused the [2004 Indian Ocean tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami "2004 Indian Ocean earthquake and tsunami").[[418]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-418)
A theory promoted by the Venezuelan state-run TV station [ViVe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ViVe "ViVe") proposed that the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake "2010 Haiti earthquake") was caused by a secret US "earthquake weapon".[[419]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-419)
### Targeted Individuals
Further information: [Gang stalking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_stalking "Gang stalking") and [Electronic harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_harassment "Electronic harassment")
Conspiracy theorists claim that government agents are utilizing [directed-energy weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directed-energy_weapon "Directed-energy weapon") and [electronic surveillance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surveillance "Surveillance") to harass members of the population. Theorists often cite research into psychotronic weapons, the [Cuban health attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Havana_syndrome "Havana syndrome"), and the [microwave auditory effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microwave_auditory_effect "Microwave auditory effect") as proof of their theory. There are more than 10,000 people who identify as "targeted individuals".[[420]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-420) The "Targeted Individual" phenomenon has been featured on episodes of _[Conspiracy Theory with Jesse Ventura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_Theory_with_Jesse_Ventura "Conspiracy Theory with Jesse Ventura")_[[421]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-421) and [History Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_\(U.S._TV_network\) "History \(U.S. TV network\)")'s [_In Search of..._](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_Search_of..._\(TV_series\) "In Search of... \(TV series\)").[[422]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-422)
### Dead Internet theory
Main article: [Dead Internet theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Internet_theory "Dead Internet theory")
The Dead Internet theory is the belief that the modern [Internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet "Internet") is almost entirely populated by [bots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_bot "Internet bot") and [procedurally generated content](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procedurally_generated_content "Procedurally generated content").[[423]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-423)
### Smartphones listening in on private conversations
See also: [Active Listening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_Listening "Active Listening")
According to the theory, smartphones with microphones listen to private off-line conversations and use the data for targeted ads.[[424]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Guardian_2021-424)[[425]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-BBC_2019-425)[[426]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Variety_2018-426)
### 5G misinformation
Main article: [5G misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G_misinformation "5G misinformation")
Conspiracy theories about [mobile phone technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_technology "Mobile technology") have circulated since the 1990s, having historical parallels with 1903-era [radiophobia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiophobia "Radiophobia").[[427]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-conv-427) With [5G technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G "5G") deploying in 2019, the same year that [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2 "SARS-CoV-2") ([the COVID-19 virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic")) was discovered, multiple conspiracy theories arose linking the two.[[427]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-conv-427)
## Sports
### Boxing
[Boxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing "Boxing") has featured in conspiracy theories, such as the claims that the second [Ali-Liston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_vs._Sonny_Liston_II "Muhammad Ali vs. Sonny Liston II") fight[[428]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-428) and the [first Bradley-Pacquiao fight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manny_Pacquiao_vs._Timothy_Bradley "Manny Pacquiao vs. Timothy Bradley") were [fixed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Match_fixing "Match fixing").[[429]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-429)
### Shergar
The theft and disappearance of the Irish-bred racehorse [Shergar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shergar "Shergar") in 1983 has prompted many conspiracy theorists to speculate about involvement by [the Mafia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicilian_Mafia "Sicilian Mafia"), the [Irish Republican Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Irish_Republican_Army "Provisional Irish Republican Army"), and [Muammar Gaddafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muammar_Gaddafi "Muammar Gaddafi").[[430]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-430)
### Rigged selection processes
The "frozen envelope theory" suggests that the [National Basketball Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Association "National Basketball Association") rigged its [1985 draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_NBA_draft "1985 NBA draft") lottery so that [Patrick Ewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Ewing "Patrick Ewing") would join the [New York Knicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Knicks "New York Knicks"). Theorists claim that a lottery envelope was chilled so that it could be identified by touch.[[431]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-431) A similar "hot balls theory", promoted by Scottish football manager [David Moyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Moyes "David Moyes"), suggests that certain balls used in draws for [UEFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA "UEFA") and [AFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Football_Confederation "Asian Football Confederation") competitions have been warmed to achieve specific outcomes.[[432]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-432)
### 1984 Firecracker 400
The [1984 Firecracker 400](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Firecracker_400 "1984 Firecracker 400") at [Daytona International Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daytona_International_Speedway "Daytona International Speedway") in [Daytona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daytona_International_Speedway "Daytona International Speedway"), Florida, was the first [NASCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASCAR "NASCAR") race to be attended by a sitting US president, [Ronald Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan"), and was driver [Richard Petty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Petty "Richard Petty")'s 200th and final career victory. Rival driver [Cale Yarborough's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cale_Yarborough "Cale Yarborough") premature retirement to the [pit road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop "Pit stop") has prompted conspiracy theorists to allege that organizers fixed the race in order to receive good publicity for the event.[[433]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-433)
### Ronaldo and the 1998 FIFA World Cup final
On the day of the [1998 FIFA World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup_final "1998 FIFA World Cup final"), Brazilian striker [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\) "Ronaldo \(Brazilian footballer\)") suffered a [convulsive fit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epileptic_seizure "Epileptic seizure").[[434]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-434) Ronaldo was initially removed from the starting lineup 72 minutes before the match, with the teamsheet released to a stunned world media, before he was reinstated by Brazil coach [Mário Zagallo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1rio_Zagallo "Mário Zagallo") shortly before kick off.[[435]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-435)[[436]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-France-436) Ronaldo "sleepwalked" through the final, with France winning the game.[[436]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-France-436) The nature of the incident set off a trail of questions and allegations that persisted for years, with [Alex Bellos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Bellos "Alex Bellos") writing in _[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")_ , "When Ronaldo's health scare was revealed after the match, the situation's unique circumstances lent itself to fabulous conspiracy theories. Here was the world's most famous sportsman, about to take part in the most important match of his career, when he suddenly, inexplicably, fell ill. Was it stress, epilepsy, or had he been drugged?"[[437]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Paris-437) Questions also circulated into who made Ronaldo play the game. Zagallo insisted he had the final say, but much speculation focused on sportswear company [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc. "Nike, Inc."), Brazil's multimillion-dollar sponsor—whom many Brazilians thought had too much control—putting pressure on the striker to play against medical advice.[[437]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-Paris-437)
### New England Patriots
See also: [New England Patriots § Controversies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Patriots#Controversies "New England Patriots")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/NRG_Stadium_before_Super_Bowl_LI.jpg/250px-NRG_Stadium_before_Super_Bowl_LI.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:NRG_Stadium_before_Super_Bowl_LI.jpg)[NRG Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NRG_Stadium "NRG Stadium") before [Super Bowl LI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LI "Super Bowl LI")
The [New England Patriots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Patriots "New England Patriots") have also been involved in numerous conspiracy theories.[[438]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-438) During their 2018 [AFC Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Championship_Game "AFC Championship Game") [24–20 victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_NFL_playoffs#AFC_Championship_Game:_New_England_Patriots_24,_Jacksonville_Jaguars_20 "2017–18 NFL playoffs") over the [Jacksonville Jaguars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville_Jaguars "Jacksonville Jaguars"), several conspiracy theories spread stating that the referees helped the Patriots advance to [Super Bowl LII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LII "Super Bowl LII") where they were eventually defeated by the champion [Philadelphia Eagles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Eagles "Philadelphia Eagles").[[439]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-439) However, sports analyst [Stephen A. Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_A._Smith "Stephen A. Smith") stated the Jaguars were not robbed, but that they had no one to blame but themselves for the loss.[[440]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-440) There were also conspiracy theories regarding the [Super Bowl LI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LI "Super Bowl LI") matchup between the Patriots and the [Atlanta Falcons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Falcons "Atlanta Falcons") stating that the game was rigged[[441]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-441) while others said the Falcons made questionable play-calls at the end of the game that resulted in them blowing a 28–3 lead.[[442]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conspiracy_theories#cite_note-442)

     * **For Helene:** Dunbar, Marina (7 October 2024).
